This inventory covers all 77 Haskell source modules under `cfta/src`,
`cfta-generator/src`, and `cfta-ghc/src` at `7bfb69d1` (PR #129).
It includes exposed and private internal modules. The five synthesis modules
and four GHC source modules have no complete formal source model.
Their presence in this inventory does not claim verified application behavior.
The GHC shared session helper is also outside formal source coverage.
The following history describes the preserved models before
the new bridges above. PR #102 adds flat intersection memo tables and interned
intersection domains. PR #103 maintains expandable and waiting UVar indexes.
Their storage and index models now have checked representation invariants.
Native publication, concrete containers, and runtime range bounds remain explicit.
Upstream rejects uncertain non-identity precedence changes
within one substitution scope and payload queries of unfinished recursive
construction occurrences. Local repair `c76d1ec` retains explicit recursive
child unions so the five-alternative pruning fixture stabilizes. That repair
is `558b316` after the latest rebase.
Grouped recursion also checks that the final body has its discovered keys
and minimum sizes before returning a tied family.
PR #97 inserts `mergeAlternatives` into every equality peer projection.
The new `MergedProjectionBound` includes this step for the complete physical
projected-leaf schedule. General recursive merge correspondence remains open;
see [merged narrowing](notes-merged-narrowing.md). Full nested free-name
semantics and the complete-record heap invariant are now source-derived;
see [free names](notes-interned-free-names.md).

Current continuation proofs add full live lexical input replay and actual
completed probe-choice sites. They derive guarded merge-call descent, physical
comparison and union language, and stabilization of cold unfolding traces in
the `PreparedWithoutMu` fragment. Cold exposure has exact finite-term semantics
in its returned cache. Ordered comparison and child selection have completion,
fuel stability, replay, and actual-cache semantic preservation. The complete
cold merge worker now derives completion and language coverage with concrete
grouping, plus exact two-fuel stability. Any hypothetical closed pending run
retains a live ordinary mismatch, its shorter source producer, both exact
current probe keys, and the earlier completed pair. The old constructor's general completion claim is refuted. Repaired
completion and nested equality fixed-point termination remain open.
Original cold-cache language transport is now proved. Exposure, comparison,
selection, and merging have pre-effect semantic statements. Complete root
row bounds include arbitrary source bucket order. Repeated source merging
on finite ordinary trees reaches an exact value-and-cache fixed point
within original ordinary height. General equality schedules still need
intervening intersection/edit progress. Exact indexed producer paths and
pending callback equations strengthen constructor provenance; general
paired-key descent cannot exclude the preserved closed pending example.
The old constructor protocol's general completion claim is now refuted by
`InternedClosedPendingBoundary`. Local source repair `e0e1b37` selects a
placeholder above the Depth observation's free ordinary names and uses the
same stored observation for readback. `InternedFreshOrdinaryConstruction`
proves freshness, minimality, and matching readback. Kernel computations
verify both repaired fixtures. Native probes and 620 source examples pass.
Repaired Facts, normalization, source origins, stored readback, root profiles,
and free-name semantic heaps are now derived. Closed completed outputs have

General repaired completion and unrestricted source correspondence remain open. Earlier
ordinary-name-specific theorems describe the preserved old executor and
need a new bridge before they establish repaired-source correspondence.
See [the repair](notes-fresh-ordinary-candidate.md) and root
[HANDOFF.md](../HANDOFF.md) for exact boundaries and evidence.

The model names below refer to files in `MicroCFTA/`.

A checked executable model is a Lean implementation with semantic theorems.
A reference model specifies behavior independently of the optimized source.
A contract states an external or representation requirement. Neither a contract
nor a passing Haskell example proves that a source algorithm meets it. This is
a source correspondence inventory, not a claim that the Haskell was extracted
from Lean. Each row states the remaining connection explicitly.

## Observable meanings

| API family | Meaning required of its results |
| --- | --- |
| `fromAutomaton`, finite or depth-bounded | One rank for each distinct accepted term. Overlapping runs must not add ranks. `DistinctRank.bounded_unique_rank` states the exact finite bijection. `Symbolic.union_count` covers the inclusion-exclusion count operation. |
| `elements`, `fromIndexed`, `pool`, choices, products, joins | One rank per retained member. Equal values, and sometimes equal constructor terms, can have different ranks. `Decoder`, `Probability`, and `Shrink` retain that multiplicity. `ranksOf` returns every matching member rank; `rankOf` returns the first. |
| `frequency`, `oneof` | Weights apply to alternatives. Equal branch weights do not make all members equiprobable when branch counts differ. `Probability.eventMass_frequency` gives this law. |
| `uniformly`, `uniformlyGrouped` | Alternatives are selected by their member counts. This policy differs from equal branch weights. `Probability.count_weighted_uniform_rank` gives the arithmetic requirement. The recursive adapters must use the matching size counts. |
| Recursive generation and `upToSize` | Size classes count members. Finite atomic choices can retain their internal weights. Unequal weights on recursive alternatives are errors; non-atomic finite weights can be replaced by member counts inside recursion. `SizeMass` states the class mass and retention laws. |
| `pmf`, `pmfAtSize` | Equal output values aggregate their masses. They do not merge replay ranks. `DistinctRank.rankMass_eq` proves the corresponding rank/term aggregation law. |
| `terms` versus bounded denotation | The suspended enumerator can stop at recursion and return markers. `plainTermsAtMost`, `denotationAtMost`, and depth-bounded imports describe bounded complete terms. `Language.mem_enumerate_iff` and `RecursiveSize.mem_enumerateAtMost_iff` use separate height and node-count bounds. |
| `minimize`, `reduce`, `minimizePoolOn` | Similarity can retain representatives and remove distinct valid terms. This is not the complete-language contract. `Guards.representative_preservation_is_not_language_equality` and `PublicResult.representatives_do_not_preserve_language` separate the meanings. In particular, `minimizePoolOn` intentionally removes less-specific pool entries. |
| `compileWith`, `validOutcomes` | Failure and solver uncertainty remain errors. A successful result must contain every admitted candidate under the supported theory. `validOutcomes` preserves candidate multiplicity; it is not a distinct-term quotient. |

A concrete source check distinguishes the first two rows: `node "x" (oneof
[pure 7, pure 7])` has two member ranks with one constructor term. Importing
its support has one term rank. See `notes-distinct-rank.md`.

## Core source modules

| Module and exported function families | Executable model or semantic contract; source boundary |
| --- | --- |
| `Data.CFTA` — `mkFTA`, `fromTerms`, accessors, `statesAt`, cycle queries, maps, `trim`, annotation, bounded enumeration, intersection, views | `FTAValidation` proves row merging, initial/child/arity validation and exact successful tables. `SourceFromTerms` proves actual sorted distinct rows and successful language/order preservation. `SourceFromTermsArity` derives the exact arity check. `SourceFiniteRebuild` and `SourceFiniteRebuildDriver` derive finite count/decoder execution and distinct compact inverse laws. `GraphTrim.trim_exact` preserves the root language of finite cyclic guarded graphs. `StateMap.map_exact` proves injective renaming; `map_complete` proves merging cannot drop an original term, although it can add terms. `Language` proves intersection and finite-run enumeration; `BoundDepth` proves depth restriction. Concrete maps, cycle detection, views and binder conversion remain representation boundaries. |
| `Data.CFTA.Constraint` — constructors, conjunction, equality extraction, residual guard, signed indicators, guard/path analysis | `ConstraintIndicators.constraint_correct` proves the complete supported Boolean guard compiler, including actual list folds, signed multiplication and residual errors. `SymbolicIndicators` gives exact path-class meaning and derives the nested graph indicator expression. `Guards.splitGuard_preserves`, `EqualityNormalize`, and `Symbolic.edgeObligations_correct` cover logical components. `EqualitySource` connects actual indexed-array normalization to exact finite-term equality and rejection. `SourceConstraintConjunction.edgeView_conjoin` derives the actual canonical semantic conjunction and equality product. `SourceGuardSplit` proves equality-free semantic output and exact current full-guard splitting in either polarity. Physical containers and guard/path-read metadata remain boundaries. Normalized PCS removes trivial classes. A reflexive `Same p p` guard still requires its path to exist. |
| `Data.CFTA.Enumeration` — `terms`, `runs`, `truncatedTerms`, plain bounded terms, pruning callbacks, expansion order, fragments | `ConservativeEmptiness` proves finite optimistic stabilization and one-sided soundness. `ConservativeEmptinessRows` derives smaller-witness decomposition for original children and literal foldr1 class requirements from three primitive laws, physical readback, and accepted-row exposure. `ConservativeEmptinessExplore` proves completed-value worklist termination, old-row retention, unique keys, and the admission bound. `ConservativeEmptinessTable*` derives the actual finite key carrier, exact raw rounds, equality-stop completion, stored-supply receipts, and literal table soundness. `SourceEmptinessKeys` and `SourceEmptinessTableSoundness` derive certified untagged record readback and exclude original guarded terms under inventory/exposure/primitive premises. `SourceEmptinessExposure` derives accepted retained rows from actual nodeEdges reads and normalized closed construction. `SourceEmptinessProjection` and `SourceEmptinessClassFold` retain plain projection and both scoped native fold schedules. `SourceEmptinessTasks`, `SourceEmptinessTaskRows`, `SourceEmptinessTaskFields`, and `SourceEmptinessTaskTrace` instantiate the local physical laws with complete row reads and captured class folds. Reached traces derive final records, complete task readout, normalized fields, exact logical keys, and inventory-free readback. Progressive lazy row demand, original child forcing, immutable captured-body receipts, saved-result language, checked native allocation transfer, machine-key adequacy, and per-call semantic soundness remain open. ### Flat mutually recursive family adapters

For `Gen.Internal.Recur`, `Recursive`, and `Query`, the `KeyedNary*` modules
extend typed family composition to arbitrary flat choices. They derive all
four grouped-record fields, callback equations, complete bounded inverses,
and candidate bounds, order, and distinctness. `KeyedNaryDispatch` follows
`mergeRecursiveGroups`, including its bare singleton case.
`KeyedNaryGuards` derives the source's combined rejection check from the
original label-aware syntax. Key identity and local finite callback
contracts remain explicit. These member laws do not quotient equal terms.

### Composite recursive join adapters

For `Gen.Internal.Chain`, `Join`, `Recursive`, `Recur`, and `Query`,
`RecursiveJoinMass` and `RecursiveJoinRecordBridge` connect heterogeneous
products to the direct source selector. `RecursiveJoinComposition` and
`RecursiveJoinFamily` derive typed nested-join families, full ordered
records, ranked distributions, minimum tables, and source equations.
`RecursiveJoinBodyFields` and `RecursiveJoinInverse` derive structural
callbacks and complete mutual inverses. `RecursiveJoinQuery` proves public
replay-filter and bounded global preimages, with bounds/order/distinctness
from local finite-leaf candidate contracts. `RecursiveJoinGuards` supplies
the original combined occurrence check. These laws retain `JoinN` wrappers;
they do not substitute ordinary `Apply` trees for join terms.

`RecursiveJoinSupportView`, `RecursiveJoinSupportBody`, and
`RecursiveJoinSupportFamily` derive complete support assembly from the typed
constructors. The actual shared `Family` binder and `AtKey` restrictions
accept exactly the independently compiled finite term derivations.
`RecursiveJoinRuntime` and `RecursiveRuntimeDispatch` connect both weight
flags, optional callback fields, bounded queries, and source error precedence.
`RecursiveRuntimeOrigins` derives those metadata fields from primitive inputs
across separate probe and term rebuilds. `RecursiveJoinInspectionBody`
derives separate diagnostic assembly and names from local descriptors.
`RecursiveUniformRuntime` and `RecursiveRuntimeVerified` derive both sampler
branches from constructor-level finite input laws. `BoundedRecursiveDispatch`
preserves the finite wrapper's empty-rank behavior when terms are missing.
`RecursiveConstructionQueries` derives exact active-token provenance and
phase-independent query rejection. Completion deletes only its owning token,
including the finite return path. This repairs a public callback whose
count and term records disagreed with its support. Opaque inspection
descriptors and arbitrary changing runtime builder schemas remain adapters.
`GroupedProbeValidation` derives final body-table equality from the source
check and proves that constructor programs retain their least-attained minima.
`RecursiveCompleteRecord` builds the supported views together and
proves that their family laws refer to the same checked compiler.
`RecursivePlainApplication` adds ordinary application with a derived
mass-free source invariant and the exact plain weight and term-field rules.
The current sparse pruning policy is also distinct from the older upfront
blanket guard in `BooleanGuardPrune` and `FullGuardPrune`. `CurrentGuardPrune` models the evaluator-first loop and proves unambiguous
cell exactness. `SourceQuotientGuards` derives full accepted ambiguous
sparse Yes transport to complete logical satisfaction, using the executed
identity map and scope checks. `CurrentGuardLogicalPrune` composes it with
the actual finite pruning loop and compiler retention.
`SourceQuotientRefutations` proves sparse No refutation for both polarities.
`CurrentGuardLogicalComplete` proves successful compiler logical exactness
and retention of every original backend-confirmed term after pruning.
Logical completeness of an original-edge fallback requires explicit backend
adequacy. `SourceReadLogicalGuards` removes exact spelling stability from
logical transport. `CurrentReadLogicalPrune` derives pruning safety,
confirmed-term retention, and successful compiler logical exactness for
unrestricted ambient values. Formula sorts still use their interpretation.
`SourceReadLogicalIntegerAdapter` derives this soundness from actual adapter
preflight and returned calls under correct external responses. Its induced
sort interpretation agrees with the independent integer model on the
complete embedded arithmetic core.

### Physical source adapters

Recent physical equality adapters derive nullary row admission, exact cache
readback, whole-node identity, Cartesian intersection, and projection union.
The source constructor checks imply the admitted canonical carrier. Raw
initial guards have a separate strict normalization-phase rank. Complete
stateful pass composition still requires finite completed-request replay.
For recursive constructors, full-syntax pending-origin and identity-footprint
proofs now identify the actual earlier reservation and complete probes.
General absent-reference replay remains open. The shallow-cache gate has a
concrete derived unfolding rank; arbitrary nested cached bodies remain open.

## Error and boundary ledger

Errors are not accepted terms and must not be counted as such. A successful
finite empty denotation differs from an unsupported operation or an unknown
solver result. The actual facade sometimes represents an empty generator as
`Left EmptyGenerator`, while reference enumeration returns an empty list.

| Error family | Concrete source cases and required interpretation |
| --- | --- |
| Empty and weighting | `EmptyGenerator`, `EmptyRanked`, nonpositive weights, insufficient weighted tickets. Reject invalid sampler domains; preserve a separate mathematical empty language. |
| Rank/value selection | Negative rank, rank beyond cardinality, `TermNotInLanguage`. No invalid rank may decode. Callback bounds remain caller obligations for indexed sources. |
| Availability | Opaque inspection, unbounded finite query, recursive term inspection without a retained term index, deferred source requiring compilation. These do not assert that the source language is empty. |
| Unsupported recursion | Weighted recursive alternatives, recursive operation family, constrained cyclic imports, ambiguous cyclic imports, unguarded recursion, bounded unresolved recursive occurrence. These are explicit limits of the current compiler, including on mathematically meaningful languages. |
| Construction consistency | `QueriedRecursiveOccurrence` rejects reads of temporary recursive payloads. `InconsistentRecursiveFamily` rejects discovered keys or minima absent from the final body. Neither error denotes an empty language. |
| Structural/codec validation | Invalid support, wrong arity, open references, recursive guard reads, missing root observation, child forest with other than one term, undecodable constructor, datatype derivation errors. Reject before relying on the corresponding representation invariant. |
| Logical/solver uncertainty | `SolverUnknown`, timeout, pruning/similarity/minimization failures, unresolved relational equality or syntactic equality, residual guards. Unknown is not a proof of rejection and must not produce a successful incomplete enumeration. |
| Integer/refinement support | Uncountable/unsupported lattice expressions, integer leaf reads that cannot be compiled, inexact measures, root condition applied to a non-constructor. The supported syntax and domain must be explicit. |

## Assessment

Every source module and exported function family has a semantic role above.
The checked development covers language semantics, distinct-term indexing,
member indexing, probability, arithmetic, and shrinking. It also contains
executable source drivers for suspended enumeration, recursive symbolic
count/select/rank, recipe compilation, pruning, and complete finite minimization.
These drivers expose errors, callbacks, caches, and operational choices.
They are more than abstract contracts. Their local theorems and composed
examples do not establish every whole-driver invariant.

The principal remaining positive proofs are finite binder-identity integration, heterogeneous runtime-family/support preservation, other recursive syntax adapters, and concrete representation correspondence. The `RecursiveCallback*` modules derive exact all-view inverses and complete bounded global rank queries for the typed single-knot binary-choice algebra. They preserve source order and distinct positions. `RecursiveNaryChoice` derives the local arbitrary-branch choice adapter from those child producers, including its independent class table and complete checked inverse. The `RecursiveNary*` modules extend typed body compilation, complete flat record equations, all-view inverses, and bounded global queries to arbitrary branch counts. Candidate rows stabilize at the requested forest height and satisfy the source knot equation; all flags retain bounds, order, and distinct positions. `StaticSamplingDispatch` and `BucketMergeDispatch` extract every finite flag branch under local constructor/field laws. The full ordered class and sampler knot equations now follow from the source size guard. `RecursiveTermAlignment`, `RecursiveTermMass`, and `RecursiveRankMass` derive value/term counts, exact forward ranks, and full ranked probability fields for typed single-knot products, choices, maps, pays, and labels, including bounded prefixes. The `KeyedTerm*` and `KeyedCallback*` modules also derive complete binary-choice mutual-family fields, source equations, and bounded global inverses. The later `KeyedNary*` and `RecursiveJoin*` modules provide the flat keyed choices and typed nested-join adapters described above. The initialized
enumeration driver now has finite-term soundness, constructive completeness,
final rendering, and sufficient stable execution bounds. The cyclic binder
builder also has total language-preserving construction under complete
cycle marking. Complete symbolic count/select/rank execution,
equality normalization, three-valued acceptance and successful pruning now
have checked source-driver theorems. Pruning still has explicit primitive
adapter and recursive termination obligations. Formula alpha-renaming
and finite sampler-record consistency now have general proofs. Explicit fuel
does not prove unbounded termination. The minimization model preserves the
selected root's accepted representatives after its repair. It does not preserve
every distinct accepted term, because it deliberately selects representatives.

Generic discovery has a finite executable worklist and whole-root language
theorem. Dynamic type allocation remains its representation bridge. Template
restriction has an exact explicit-state product proof. Finite generator
composition derives member counts, ranks, grouping coverage, and masses.
The API distinction between distinct terms and retained members remains
necessary; equal values need not have unique member ranks.

External contracts are narrower: Haskell primitive/runtime behavior, total
user codecs/index callbacks, lawful random draws, and sound solver answers.
Diagnostic rendering and user effects do not prove semantic correctness.
No source algorithm is marked correct solely because tests pass. No generic
contract substitutes for an unproved internal algorithm invariant.
